## 2. Hard rules (no exceptions)

- NEVER access, ping, scan or connect to **192.168.5.52**, **192.168.5.53** (production; the SVN server is on .52) or **192.168.5.15** (shared Keycloak / `ERPSystem`) — also not indirectly through a test default or a configuration value. Only local SVN commands: `svn status`, `svn info`, `svn diff` / `svn cat` against BASE. **No `svn update`, no `svn commit`** (the owner commits).
- **No file deletion, rename or `svn rm`** without explicit owner approval in chat for that task. Always back up a file **before** editing it (agent scratchpad).
- Never touch `_cleanup_review/` (do not even scan into it). No deployment, no production / server access. No destructive DB action except dropping the exact test databases your own run created.
- No passwords, tokens or secrets in code, logs, documents or chat output. Credentials only from environment variables, read in memory.
- **ACCOUNT-BOOK:** only SaaS-tenant / security plumbing may change; business "Organization" logic (`organization_profile`, `bank_accounts.organization_id`, business services) never changes. Its **94 unit-test failures (30 failures + 64 errors) are a KNOWN BASELINE — never fix them**, report them separately; the failing list must stay identical.
- `CMP_<n>` (`^CMP_[0-9]{1,8}$`) is the only company identifier; realm `co-<n>`. `ORG_*`, `org_`, `X-Organization-Id`, `ORG_MODULES` are retired — never reintroduce them.
- **No commits until all phases are complete (CD-67, owner, 2026-10-13):** never run `svn commit`, `svn add` or any SVN command that changes repository or working-copy state; never ask the owner to commit or merge before continuing; a task's DONE status never means "committed" — every task stays uncommitted until the owner's final service-wise commit after all phases.
- Tasks finish as **REVIEW, never DONE**. Claim a task in the tracker (IN_PROGRESS + agent + date) **before** working. Update `PHASE_STATUS_TRACKER.md` and append `WORK_LOG.md` after each task; update `AGENT_HANDOFF.md` / this prompt. **Never start the next task without the owner's explicit approval.**
- If an owner decision is needed or a production-code change is unclear, STOP and ask; do not invent architecture decisions. **Do not change D3-10** (open).

## 3. Local environment

- **This workstation is 192.168.5.141.**
- **MySQL** (CD-29): `192.168.5.141:3306`, credentials = root `.env` `DB_*` (never print / write / change them). Pass only `-Dit.mysql.host=192.168.5.141 -Dit.mysql.allowed-host=192.168.5.141 -Dit.mysql.port=…`. **The user and the password are never passed by the runner: the ITs read them only from the root `.env` (`DB_USER` / `DB_PASSWORD`, the single place) through the COMMON test-jar `ItMySqlCredentials`; never `-Dit.mysql.user` / `-Dit.mysql.password`** — Surefire writes every system property into `TEST-*.xml`, Hibernate logs them, and the local user name equals the password; since 2026-10-12 every IT fails at once when either property is set (P06-T6 review finding N3). After every run: `powershell -NoProfile -File test-tools\check-test-secrets.ps1` (reads `.env` itself; exit 0 required; prints paths only, never values). Before every run: `SHOW DATABASES` pre-flight; **protected: information_schema, mysql, performance_schema, sys, sakila, world**. Afterwards drop only the run-created DBs (pattern `^(cmp_[0-9]{1,8}_[a-z_]+_db|control_panel_[a-z_]+|parity_[a-z])$`, plus `it_routing_capacity`, `it_routing_org101`, `it_routing_org102`, which three COMMON unit tests create on every unit run when `-Dit.mysql.*` is set). Clean between two IT runs (fixed-name CP test DBs fail on re-run).
- **Redis:** Docker `erp-it-redis` (`redis:7-alpine`, created 2026-10-07 with owner approval, `--restart unless-stopped`) on `127.0.0.1:16379` (`-Dit.redis.host=127.0.0.1 -Dit.redis.port=16379`); Docker Desktop must be running; the former `it-redis-p02` no longer exists — if `erp-it-redis` is missing, ask the owner before creating it. The local Keycloak 25.0.6 runs natively (java on 8081), not in Docker. MySQL: `IT_MYSQL_*` environment variables are not set — use the root `.env` `DB_*` mapping (host must be 192.168.5.141), in memory only. Snapshot the key set before an IT run; afterwards delete only the keys the run added (usually `TENANT_META:CMP_<test id>:*`, ~78).
- **Keycloak = 25.0.6, FINAL for local AND live (owner decision CD-30; no 26.x).** Local install `C:\Software\Keycloak\keycloak-25.0.6\keycloak-25.0.6` (`start-dev --http-port 8081`), use **only `http://127.0.0.1:8081`**. You may start / stop only this local installation. Admin login only via the user environment variables `LOCAL_KC_ADMIN_USER` / `LOCAL_KC_ADMIN_PASSWORD` (read with `[Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariable(name,'User')`; never print). The root `.env` `KEYCLOAK_*` point to the shared 192.168.5.15 — never use them.
- **Local test realms — KEEP, never delete:** `co-101` (CMP_101, ACTIVE in tests), `co-102` (CMP_102, DISABLED case), `co-103` (CMP_103, unregistered), `it-platform` (platform stand-in). Test-only helper clients live only inside them: `it-token-client`, `it-mismatch-client`, `it-noaud-client`, `it-short-client`, `it-spoof-client`, `it-platform-client`, `it-platform-admin-client`, user `it-user`, and since P03-T7 the M2M clients `svc-control-panel-service`, `svc-crm-service`, `svc-it-noaud-client`, `svc-it-short-client` in `it-platform`; since P03-T11 the service accounts `it-platform-identity-admin` (in `it-platform`) and `it-realm-provisioner` (in `master`, role `create-realm`). The P03-T11 IT creates a temporary realm `co-104` (CMP_104) and deletes it again — the only realm a test may delete. Fixture: COMMON test-jar `nyaalkaran.erp.common.security.testsupport.LocalKeycloak` (`ensureHelpers()` creates them idempotently).
- **Gated real-Keycloak ITs** (run only when `LOCAL_KC_ADMIN_USER` is set; on a fresh Keycloak run CP `LocalKeycloakRealmTemplateIT` first): COMMON `LocalKeycloakInternalServiceAuthenticationIT`; CP `LocalKeycloakRealmTemplateIT`, `LocalKeycloakRealmRegistryIT`, `LocalKeycloakControlPlaneIssuerIT`, `LocalKeycloakIdentityAdminIT` (P03-T11), `LocalKeycloakLoginDiscoveryIT` (P03-T12, read-only), `LocalKeycloakRealmReconcilerIT` (P03-T13; creates and deletes the temporary realm `co-105` and master client `it-realm-auditor`), `LocalKeycloakPlatformBootstrapIT` (P03-T2; creates and deletes the temporary platform realm `it-platform-t2`); gateway `LocalKeycloakGatewayIT`; `CrmLocalKeycloakTokenIT`, `AccountBookLocalKeycloakTokenIT`, `NotificationLocalKeycloakTokenIT` (12 classes).

Unit: `mvn -o -fae clean install` (whole reactor, ~20 min). ITs: `mvn -o -fae install -Dtest=*IT -Dsurefire.failIfNoSpecifiedTests=false` + `-Dit.*` (one reactor with COMMON works). Install COMMON (`mvn -o -q install -pl COMMON-LIBRARY -DskipTests`) after changing it before building a single other module. Do not edit a module's sources while a reactor run has not yet compiled that module.

LISTED list filtering / archive = P04-T5 (not started); `/api/v1/platform/companies/{code}/**` = PHASE_10; role-policy editing deferred (D7); D3-10 remains OPEN; P04-T5 and every other task wait for an explicit owner go.
1l. **P05-T3 (owner) — DONE (2026-10-07):** P05-T3 is **DONE** (owner approval "P05-T3 OWNER APPROVAL — MARK DONE" after the read-only review REVIEW PASS; owner decisions P-1..P-12; WORK_LOG "P05-T3 implementation" + "P05-T3 owner approval → DONE"). Non-blocking review observations (retained, not resolved; no approved rule requires otherwise): (a) `selectable` for a DRAFT plan returns the company's ACTIVE contract version; (b) a contract version can be created for a company regardless of the company status; (c) `PlanService.save` maps every DB constraint violation to the generic "A plan with this code exists" message. Earlier implementation notes (from the REVIEW, kept): (1) a contract version can be published while its plan is still DRAFT (P-2 activates a plan only via a standard publish), so the one-ACTIVE-plan rule covers the standard activation only; (2) retiring the last ACTIVE standard version leaves the plan ACTIVE without a current standard version; (3) plan creation takes no target-group lock (DRAFT plans unrestricted); (4) existing COMMON `GlobalExceptionHandler` answers an unsupported HTTP method with 500 (reported, not changed). Next in the PHASE_05 order after the review: P05-T6 (only with an explicit owner go); P05-T7 = P06-T8.
1m. **P05-T6 (owner) — DONE (2026-10-08) — decisions and findings:** TX-1..TX-11 approved exactly as recommended (plan T6: **TX-1** when a new APPROVED version starts on date D, the previous open-ended APPROVED version automatically gets `effective_to` = D − 1 — the only permitted modification of an APPROVED version, a controlled, audited supersede; otherwise APPROVED versions stay immutable; **TX-2** `effective_from` must be ≥ the approval date (no back-dated approval; future dates allowed); **TX-3** RETIRE: DRAFT versions and APPROVED versions not yet effective may be retired; the currently effective APPROVED version cannot be retired directly — it is superseded by its successor (TX-1); **TX-4** tax-rule matching specificity: (1) `sac_code` + `plan_type`, (2) `plan_type`, (3) `sac_code`, (4) generic — the most specific matching APPROVED rule wins; **TX-5** `components_json`: INTRA = CGST + SGST with equal rates; INTER = IGST only; EXPORT / SEZ = IGST, 0 % valid; rates 0..100, scale ≤ 4; cess optional, ≥ 0; **TX-6** the ACCOUNT-BOOK `GstTreatment` values (no new value set): OVERSEAS → EXPORT, SEZ → SEZ, all other treatments → seller state vs place-of-supply state decides INTRA / INTER; REGISTERED_* → B2B, all others → B2C; **TX-7** R1: one APPROVED default seller entity per date (`is_default` unique among APPROVED seller entities for a date); several seller codes may exist; gate and GST resolver use the default seller; **TX-8** SAMPLE configuration only under the profile `sample`, never under `prod` / `production`: seller `state_code` `24`; tax rules INTRA CGST 9 + SGST 9 and INTER IGST 18, SAC NULL; billing policy ON_FULL_PAYMENT, HALF_UP, LINE, `round_off_unit` 1.00; no other SAMPLE tax values; **TX-9** go-live gate in T6: APPROVED seller entity, APPROVED billing policy and APPROVED tax configuration (≥ 1 APPROVED INTRA rule and ≥ 1 APPROVED INTER rule) for the date; no document series (PHASE_07); production = profile `prod` or `production`; startup WARN when not approved; no dashboard / status endpoint; missing configuration in production → 409 `TAX_CONFIGURATION_NOT_APPROVED`; no "tax not configured" override; **TX-10** for TaxRule, SellerEntity and BillingPolicy: list, get, create DRAFT, update DRAFT, approve, retire; no DELETE; v1 `@PlatformSuperAdminOnly`, v2 `platform.tax.manage` on every endpoint, no new permission; dedicated `TaxConfigAudit` with `TAX_RULE_` / `SELLER_ENTITY_` / `BILLING_POLICY_` + `CREATED` / `UPDATED` / `APPROVED` / `RETIRED`, SUCCESS and FAILED; version = max + 1 per code (TaxRule, SellerEntity), one global sequence (BillingPolicy); shared audit base classes unchanged; **TX-11** GSTIN 15 characters with the first 2 = `state_code`; PAN 10 characters; `state_code` exactly 2 digits; `fy_start_month` 1..12; `amount_scale` 0..4; `rounding_mode` = a Java `RoundingMode` name; `round_off_unit` > 0 or NULL). Second readiness check NOT READY — open: **TX-12** (tax-rule identity): what is the "rule key" for the one-APPROVED-per-date rule and for the TX-1 supersede — the `code` (CD-25 "one APPROVED version per object and date", TX-10 "version per code") or the applicability + supply type (PHASE_01 `tbl_tax_rule` "exactly one APPROVED rule per (applicability, supply type, date)")? The two differ when versions of one code change `sac_code` / `plan_type` / `supply_type` or two codes share one applicability; recommended: a code's applicability (`sac_code`, `plan_type`) and `supply_type` are fixed for all its versions (set by the first version, 409 on a different value), and two codes with the same applicability + supply type must not have overlapping APPROVED windows (409) — so both statements hold and TX-1 supersedes within the code; **TX-13** (SAMPLE usage outside production): the plan / PHASE_07 say the engine uses the "APPROVED (or SAMPLE in dev)" configuration and PDFs are watermarked when a SAMPLE / non-APPROVED version is used, but no rule says when the resolver may use the DRAFT SAMPLE rows, and the SAMPLE rows need an `effective_from` (NOT NULL); recommended: outside `prod` / `production` only, when no APPROVED version covers the date, the resolver may use the DRAFT `SAMPLE-*` versions covering the date and marks the result `sample = true` (watermark input for PHASE_07); production uses APPROVED only; SAMPLE rows get `effective_from` = the seeding date, no `effective_to`; SAMPLE names: codes and seller `legal_name` `SAMPLE-*`. **TX-12 APPROVED** (owner, 2026-10-07, exactly as recommended): tax-rule identity = applicability (`sac_code`, `plan_type`) + `supply_type`; these fields are fixed by the first version of a tax-rule code — a later version with a different applicability or `supply_type` → 409; two different codes with the same applicability + `supply_type` must not have overlapping APPROVED effective windows → 409; the TX-1 supersede applies only within the same code; TX-10 version numbering stays per code. **TX-13 APPROVED** (owner, 2026-10-07, exactly as recommended): `SAMPLE-*` DRAFT tax-rule versions may be used only when the active runtime profile is not `prod` / `production`, and only when no APPROVED tax-rule version exists for the requested date; `prod` / `production` use APPROVED configuration only, SAMPLE is never selected; SAMPLE resolver results expose `sample = true` (PHASE_07 watermark); SAMPLE `effective_from` = the seed / creation date, `effective_to` NULL unless explicitly retired; SAMPLE tax-rule codes and the seller `legal_name` follow `SAMPLE-*`; no other SAMPLE fallback behaviour. Third readiness check NOT READY — open: **TX-14** (SAMPLE seller outside production): TX-7 says the GST resolver uses the APPROVED default seller entity valid on the date, and TX-13 allows a SAMPLE fallback for tax-rule versions only ("no other SAMPLE fallback behaviour"); TX-8 seeds the SAMPLE seller (state `24`) as a DRAFT row. Outside production no APPROVED seller exists, so the resolver cannot determine the seller state and the TX-13 SAMPLE tax-rule path can never be reached — the decisions contradict each other. Recommended: outside `prod` / `production` only, when no APPROVED default seller entity covers the date, the resolver may use the DRAFT `SAMPLE-*` default seller entity covering the date and marks the result `sample = true`; production uses the APPROVED default seller only. The SAMPLE billing policy is only seeded in T6 (no T6 consumer; the gate checks APPROVED only); any billing-policy SAMPLE use and its identification (`tbl_billing_policy` has no code / name column) is decided with P05-T5. **TX-14 APPROVED** (owner, 2026-10-07, exactly as recommended): outside `prod` / `production` only, when no APPROVED default seller entity exists for the requested date, the resolver may use the DRAFT `SAMPLE-*` default seller entity; `prod` / `production` use the APPROVED seller configuration only, the SAMPLE seller is never selected; a result that uses the SAMPLE seller exposes `sample = true`; the SAMPLE billing policy is seeded by T6 but not used by any T6 resolver logic; the production gate checks the APPROVED billing policy only; SAMPLE billing-policy usage and its identification are deferred to P05-T5; no additional SAMPLE fallback behaviour. **Fourth readiness check (2026-10-07, after recording TX-14): PASS** — TX-1..TX-14 cover the T6 rules without conflict; dependencies P05-T1..T3 DONE; no schema change. Literal readings applied (non-blocking): "no APPROVED tax-rule version exists for the requested date" (TX-13) and "no APPROVED default seller exists for the requested date" (TX-14) are evaluated for the date as written (any APPROVED tax-rule version of that date blocks the SAMPLE tax-rule path); the SAMPLE billing policy is seeded only into an empty `tbl_billing_policy` (its identification is deferred to P05-T5, TX-14); seed rows are not audited (no admin mutation, as the existing catalog seeders). **P05-T6 claimed IN_PROGRESS 2026-10-07.** **Implemented 2026-10-07 → REVIEW** (WORK_LOG "P05-T6 implementation"; plan T6 "Status 2026-10-07: REVIEW"). Review notes for the owner (design points within TX-1..TX-14): (1) approvals lock their scope first — tax rule: the code + the same identity, seller / billing policy: all versions — concurrent approvals have exactly one winner; (2) the TX-1 supersede is audited as `*_UPDATED` SUCCESS with `reason = SUPERSEDED`; (3) in production the resolver calls the gate first (full APPROVED configuration required, 409 `TAX_CONFIGURATION_NOT_APPROVED`); (4) TX-13 literal reading: any APPROVED tax rule valid on the date disables the SAMPLE rules for every supply type on that date; (5) SAMPLE seller + APPROVED rule → `sample = true`; (6) `PUT` replaces a DRAFT, the code is not in the body; (7) concurrent creates of one version → 409 duplicate (retry); (8) no runtime consumer of the resolver in T6 (P05-T5 / PHASE_07); (9) SAC codes checked for length only. **Final owner review 2026-10-08: PASS** (no blocking defect) → **DONE** (owner approval 2026-10-08 "P05-T6 owner review is APPROVED … Mark P05-T6 = DONE"; no implementation change). Non-blocking findings retained as observations / follow-ups (not fixed in T6; each needs a separate owner decision): (F-1) TX-12 concurrency: `TaxRuleService.create` reads the first version without a lock and `update` locks only its own row — an identity-changing update of a code's only DRAFT version and the simultaneous creation of its version 2 can both succeed (versions of one code with different identities); `approve` does not re-check the identity against the first version; not covered by the concurrency ITs — follow-up candidate (lock the code's versions in create / update, or re-check at approval); (F-2) `SAMPLE-*` rows can be updated / approved through the API; once APPROVED they count as real configuration (gate passes, `sample = false`) — no TX rule covers it, a guard needs a new owner decision; (F-3) TX-3 × TX-1: retiring a future APPROVED successor leaves the superseded predecessor ending on D − 1 (TX-1 forbids reopening it) — a coverage gap from D until a new version is approved (production 409 for those dates); (F-4) the TX-13 literal reading is global: any APPROVED tax rule valid on the date disables the SAMPLE rules for that date for every supply type (e.g. dev with only an APPROVED INTRA rule: INTER / EXPORT / SEZ answer 409, never SAMPLE); per-supply-type behaviour would need a new decision; (F-5) TX-6 literal: SEZ and OVERSEAS are B2C (only REGISTERED_* are B2B) although SEZ units are normally GST-registered — revisit if needed; (F-6) the TX-9 gate counts any APPROVED INTRA / INTER rule incl. SAC- or plan-specific ones, so a generic resolution can still answer 409 while the gate passes — as decided; (F-7) test gaps: no resolver test of the seller supersede boundary D − 1 / D (the same query is proven for tax rules); the concurrency ITs assert the final state, not lock contention; the startup WARN text is not asserted; "no SAMPLE rows on a normal startup" is proven only by the `@Profile("sample")` test; (F-8) environment, unrelated to T6: ACCOUNT-BOOK unit tests write 6 files per reactor run into the unversioned `ACCOUNT-BOOK-SERVICE/uploads/` (since 2026-10-02) — left unchanged.
1n. **P05-T4 (owner) — DONE (2026-10-08) — decisions and observations:** D4-1..D4-6 approved exactly as recommended after the read-only T4 readiness review (NOT READY): **D4-1** T4 = the discount SPI, the MANUAL source, value types, validation and pure unit tests only; no pricing / calculation engine in T4; engine integration tests, line / order calculation and the S8-R1 / S9 engine behaviour belong to P05-T5; T4 is not merged into T5 and no temporary / mini engine is built; **D4-2** `DiscountSource` provides discount instructions only — level (LINE / ORDER), type, value, reason, approvedBy, sequence; it never calculates monetary amounts, rounding or allocation (exclusively the P05-T5 engine); `DiscountSourceType` = MANUAL, CATALOG, only MANUAL implemented in T4; **D4-3** T4 defines a pure `ManualAdjustment` value / record in `platform/pricing/engine`; no persistence entity, table, Liquibase migration, endpoint / API or audit in T4; `tbl_billing_order_discount`, the `platform.order.discount` endpoint, persistence and audit belong to PHASE_07; T4 does not modify the PHASE_07 schema; **D4-4** PERCENT: 0 < value ≤ 100, scale ≤ 4; FIXED: value > 0, scale ≤ 4; zero values invalid; reason mandatory, trimmed, at most 500 characters; `approvedByIdentityId` optional; R1 permits at most one line adjustment per line and at most one order adjustment per order; line adjustments before the order adjustment; FIXED values are expressed in the order currency; a line adjustment may apply to any line type with a positive gross; a discount exceeding its applicable base is rejected with an explicit validation error, never capped — the monetary / base check stays in the T5 engine, T4 validates only the adjustment instruction itself; **D4-5** no second-approver requirement; `approvedByIdentityId` optional; T4 performs no approval authorization / check; `platform.order.discount` remains the PHASE_07 endpoint permission; no new permission; **D4-6** the MANUAL adjustment has no cap field; S8-R1 uses a simple percentage MANUAL adjustment without a cap; coupon caps belong to the PHASE_14 coupons; no cap field and no PHASE_07 schema change. **Second readiness check (2026-10-08, after recording D4-1..D4-6): PASS** — D4-1..D4-6 resolve the T4 gaps of the first check (engine-dependent tests, SPI responsibility, adjustment ownership, validation rules, approval semantics, S8 cap); no conflict with TX-1..TX-14, CD-10, CD-21, F5-5 or F5-6; dependencies met (T6 DONE; under D4-1 / D4-3 T4 needs no P05-T5 or PHASE_07 artifact); no schema / Liquibase change; no endpoint, permission, audit, MySQL, Redis or Keycloak dependency (pure unit tests; CP regression on local infrastructure only). Technical implementation assumptions (no business rule; derived from PHASE_01 and the existing code patterns; the owner may adjust them with the authorization): **A-1** a LINE adjustment references its line by the order line number (`line_no` of PHASE_01 `tbl_billing_order_line`, a positive integer) — the T5 engine maps it to its lines; an ORDER adjustment has no line number; **A-2** the instruction `sequence` is assigned deterministically: LINE adjustments in ascending line number, then the ORDER adjustment (D4-4); **A-3** an invalid instruction (incl. a second adjustment on one line or a second order adjustment) raises a dedicated 400 error code in the existing `ApiException` pattern (e.g. `INVALID_DISCOUNT_ADJUSTMENT`); **A-4** the `platform/pricing/engine` classes are plain Java (no Spring, JPA or repository dependency); a purity unit test checks their class files with the existing constant-pool scanning technique of the COMMON architecture scanners (F5-5; no new test dependency); the source receives its input through a small context value type that the T5 engine builds from its request. **Not claimed; P05-T4 stays PENDING until the owner explicitly authorizes implementation.** **Authorized and claimed 2026-10-08** (A-1..A-4 approved, A-3 clarified: pure domain validation error, no web-layer dependency) → **implemented → REVIEW 2026-10-08** (WORK_LOG "P05-T4 implementation"). Non-blocking observations: (O-1) the scale rule ignores trailing zeros (`12.345600` accepted, as the TX-5 rates); the value keeps its input scale — PHASE_07 persistence (`DECIMAL(19,4)`) normalizes it; (O-2) FIXED has no upper / integer-digit bound in T4 — the base check is the T5 engine's (D4-4), the column bound PHASE_07's; (O-3) `approvedByIdentityId` is not validated (any value) — D4-5 no check; (O-4) `DiscountContext` carries only the MANUAL adjustments; future CATALOG / coupon inputs extend this record additively (the SPI method signature stays); (O-5) the purity rule allows every `java/*` type (no narrower allowlist); (O-6) the engine classes are not Spring beans — P05-T5 instantiates / wires `ManualAdjustmentDiscountSource`. **Owner review 2026-10-08: PASS** (no blocking defect) → **DONE** (owner approval 2026-10-08 "P05-T4 owner review is APPROVED … Mark P05-T4 = DONE"; no implementation change; D4-1..D4-6, A-1..A-4 unchanged). Observations / follow-ups retained (not fixed in T4; each needs a separate owner decision): (O-1) the scale rule ignores trailing zeros (`12.345600` accepted, as the approved TX-5 rates); the value keeps its input scale — not reopened in T4: the interpretation stays consistent with the approved TX-5 approach, and literal BigDecimal-scale semantics would need a separate owner decision; (O-2) FIXED has no upper / integer-digit bound in T4 — the base check is the T5 engine's (D4-4), the column bound PHASE_07's; (O-3) `approvedByIdentityId` is not validated — D4-5 no check; (O-4) `DiscountContext` carries only the MANUAL adjustments; future CATALOG / coupon inputs extend it additively (the SPI method signature stays); (O-5) the purity rule allows every `java/*` type (the classes use only `java.lang` / `java.util` / `java.math`); (O-6) the engine classes are not Spring beans — P05-T5 instantiates / wires `ManualAdjustmentDiscountSource`; (R-1) `String.trim()` removes only ASCII whitespace, so a reason of only Unicode spaces (e.g. U+00A0, U+3000) counts as non-blank — as `TaxConfigValidator`; not fixed in T4; (R-2) nothing maps `DiscountValidationException` to HTTP 400 yet — by design (no endpoint, D4-3); the future API boundary (PHASE_07 / T5) must map it to 400 `INVALID_DISCOUNT_ADJUSTMENT`, otherwise the current `GlobalExceptionHandler` would answer 500; not implemented in T4. T5 notes: billing-policy rounding (TX-11) vs §6.5 HALF_UP; TX-14 SAMPLE billing-policy use / identification; the engine tests moved from T4 by D4-1 (line / order adjustments, S8-R1, S9, base check of D4-4); wiring of `ManualAdjustmentDiscountSource` (O-6); the 400 mapping of R-2 at the API boundary.
1o. **P05-T5 (owner) — DONE (2026-10-08) — decisions and observations:** S5-1..S5-14 approved exactly as recommended, with authoritative clarifications, after the read-only T5 readiness review (NOT READY): **S5-1** a duplicate within the same order / request: the duplicate line is dropped with the warning `DUPLICATE_ENTITLEMENT` (S3); a duplicate against an already-active entitlement, regardless of subscription: the request is rejected with `DUPLICATE_ENTITLEMENT` (S10, PHASE_06 acceptance 2); `REDUNDANT_BUNDLE`: the request is rejected (the bundle is not merely dropped); the distinction holds consistently in engine behaviour and tests; **S5-2** CONDITIONAL dependencies are informational only in R1: never auto-added, never charged, a warning when applicable; they do not contribute to the S4 / S5 totals; **S5-3** authoritative T5 calculation order (supersedes conflicting earlier wording on proration / discount / tax ordering): (1) expand and validate dependencies, (2) line gross = quantity × unit price, (3) proration applied to gross, (4) line discounts, (5) order discount, (6) the order discount allocated by largest remainder proportional to the net line amounts, (7) taxable amounts, (8) tax per line and per tax component incl. cess, (9) other charges = 0 in R1, (10) round-off, (11) final totals; proration: factor = remaining / period, remaining = period_end − change_date + 1, period = the actual number of days of the subscription billing period, factor scale 6, HALF_UP; S6 must produce 15 / 30; **S5-4** the APPROVED billing policy controls `rounding_mode` and `amount_scale`; HALF_UP is only the default, never hard-coded where the policy supplies another mode (authoritative over the fixed HALF_UP wording); the configured rounding applies to each line gross, discount, allocation and tax component; totals are sums of the rounded amounts; **S5-5** `tax_rounding_level` LINE: every tax component of every line rounded independently; INVOICE: each tax component calculated unrounded across all applicable lines, aggregated at order level, rounded once, then the rounded component total allocated back to the lines by deterministic largest remainder; tests cover both modes; **S5-6** `round_off_unit` NULL = no round-off; otherwise rounding to the nearest multiple of `round_off_unit` with the policy `rounding_mode`; round-off is non-taxable and happens after tax and before the final `grand_total`; **S5-7** production (`prod` / `production`) never uses a SAMPLE billing policy — the gate requires the APPROVED policy; outside production, when no APPROVED billing policy exists for the requested date, T5 may use the exact TX-8 SAMPLE billing-policy values from code constants, never identifying a SAMPLE DB row; then `result.sample = true`; T5 never creates or persists SAMPLE billing-policy rows (resolves the TX-14 deferral); **S5-8** `PricingRequest` explicitly contains `gstTreatment` and `placeOfSupplyState`; T5 does not depend on the current incomplete `BillingAccount`; PHASE_07 may later fill these values from the billing account; **S5-9** SAC: `plan_price.sac_code`, else the seller `default_sac_code`, else null; plan type: dependency / module line MODULE, additional-user line USER_SEAT, additional-storage line STORAGE; these values go to the approved T6 tax resolver; **S5-10** USER_SEAT: the ACTIVE USER_SEAT plan's `included_users` plus the selected lines' included users; additional users priced as additional quantity × `unit_price`; selected users ≤ `max_quantity`; STORAGE: blocks = ceil((selected storage − included storage) / `unit_quantity`); selected storage ≤ `max_quantity`; no applicable USER_SEAT / STORAGE plan and the request exceeds the included amount → validation error; **S5-11** the company's ACTIVE contract version takes precedence, otherwise the ACTIVE standard version; the plan itself must be ACTIVE; requests identify plans by plan code; DRAFT versions are never used for pricing; **S5-12** the pure engine supports NEW, UPGRADE and DOWNGRADE inputs; existing entitlement / period data are explicit engine inputs — T5 does not resolve subscription persistence; DOWNGRADE validates and schedules the change for the end of the current period with no immediate charge; T5's public calculate endpoint is NEW calculation only; PHASE_06 / 07 resolve actual subscription data later; **S5-13** endpoint v1 `@PlatformSuperAdminOnly`, v2 `platform.billing.read`; no persistence-level manual adjustments through this endpoint; manual adjustments are consumed by the pure engine through the T4 SPI; order-discount persistence / API belongs to PHASE_07 under `platform.order.discount`; no new permission; **S5-14** the S1–S7 golden tests validate subtotal / taxable values separately from tax (the "Total (IU)" values are not tax-inclusive); tax has dedicated tests; S8 / S9 validate the full tax-inclusive total where applicable. **Second readiness check (2026-10-08, after recording S5-1..S5-14): PASS** — S5-1..S5-14 resolve the gaps and conflicts of the first check; no further business-rule gap; no conflict with TX-1..TX-14, D4-1..D4-6, A-1..A-4, CD-07 / CD-10 / CD-21 / CD-25, Q-04 (non-tax), Q-08, Q-10, P-1..P-12, T6-3 / T6-4, F5-5 / F5-6; dependencies met (T1, T2, T3, T4, T6 DONE; under S5-8 / S5-12 no PHASE_06 / PHASE_07 artifact is needed); no schema / Liquibase change. Technical implementation assumptions (no business rule; the owner may adjust them with the authorization): **A5-1** the pure engine lives in `platform/pricing/engine` (plain Java, covered by the T4 purity test); the input resolution service and `PricingController` live outside it (e.g. `platform/pricing/quote`); **A5-2** request: `companyCode` (required — R1 orders always have a company), billing period, currency, plan codes (MODULE / BUNDLE), users, storage GB, `gstTreatment`, `placeOfSupplyState`, optional pricing date (default today, server clock); no adjustments (S5-13); **A5-3** the engine raises plain-Java domain errors (A-3 pattern) mapped at the endpoint: 400 `INVALID_PRICING_REQUEST` (limits, S5-10 missing USER_SEAT / STORAGE plan, invalid input), 400 `INVALID_DISCOUNT_ADJUSTMENT` (incl. a discount exceeding its base, D4-4; R-2 resolved at this boundary), 409 `DUPLICATE_ENTITLEMENT`, 409 `REDUNDANT_BUNDLE`, 409 `PRICE_NOT_AVAILABLE` (no ACTIVE plan / version / price for a code, currency or period, incl. a MANDATORY dependency module), 409 `TAX_CONFIGURATION_NOT_APPROVED` (T6), 409 `ROUNDING_NOT_POSSIBLE` (an approved `rounding_mode` UNNECESSARY where rounding is needed — allowed by TX-11); **A5-4** largest remainder: units of 10^−amount_scale, remainders descending, ties by ascending line number; INVOICE-level allocation proportional to the unrounded line component amounts; **A5-5** result: lines (line number, type, plan / version / price references, module / bundle, quantity, unit price, period, proration factor, gross, line discount, allocated order discount, taxable, tax components, line total, SAC), applied discount instructions with amounts, tax totals per component, warnings (`DUPLICATE_ENTITLEMENT`, `BUNDLE_OVERLAP`, `RECOMMENDED_DEPENDENCY_MISSING`, `CONDITIONAL_DEPENDENCY`), totals (subtotal, discount, taxable, tax, other charges 0, round-off, grand total), `sample`, seller code / version, billing-policy version or SAMPLE, `calculation_version` `PE-1`; **A5-6** a dependency line is priced with the dependency module's ACTIVE MODULE plan under S5-11; INCLUDED → zero-price line; **A5-7** UPGRADE prices the new lines and the increase of additional users / storage blocks over the explicit existing inputs, prorated per S5-3; DOWNGRADE validates with explicit inputs (dependants, active billable users, storage usage — master §12.4) and returns a zero-amount schedule effective the day after the current period end; **A5-8** tests: pure unit / property tests (S1–S12, S5-1..S5-14 incl. both tax-rounding levels, round-off NULL / 1.00, rounding modes, proration boundaries, dependency chains, determinism, totals = Σ lines, allocations = discount, components = tax total), MySQL IT on 192.168.5.141 for the input resolution (contract precedence, ACTIVE only, SAMPLE policy fallback outside production, production gate), endpoint security test (v1 / v2, 403 + one denial audit), no DB concurrency test (no persistence), regression CP unit / CP IT / full reactor / ACCOUNT-BOOK 94 names. Non-blocking observations: (N-1) the NEW-only endpoint does not resolve existing entitlements, so a preview cannot detect a duplicate against an active entitlement — PHASE_06 / 07 do (S5-12); (N-2) the tax values stay PENDING with the CA (SAMPLE outside production, production gated); (N-3) T6 F-6: the gate can pass while a specific line answers 409; (N-4) for pricing, S5-11 (plan must be ACTIVE) supersedes the P05-T3 observation that `selectable` returns contract versions of a DRAFT plan. **Not claimed; P05-T5 stays PENDING until the owner explicitly authorizes implementation.** **Authorized and claimed 2026-10-08** (A5-1..A5-8 approved) → **implemented → REVIEW 2026-10-08** (WORK_LOG "P05-T5 implementation"). ## 8. Things to know (gotchas)

- **2026-10-12 lessons (P06-T6 follow-ups / P06-T6-F1):** (1) **test DB login only from the root `.env`** via the COMMON test-jar `ItMySqlCredentials` — never `-Dit.mysql.user` / `-Dit.mysql.password` (Surefire writes system properties into `TEST-*.xml`, Hibernate logs them; the local user name equals the password); any IT fails if either property is set; run `test-tools\check-test-secrets.ps1` before and after every run (exit 0 required; prints paths only). (2) The COMMON test-jar is filtered by `<includes>` — a new shared test helper must be added there (reactor `test-compile` hides a missing include; a module IT then fails with `NoClassDefFoundError`). (3) Never pipe the IT runner into `Select-Object -First` / `head` — it stops the script before its DB / Redis cleanup; write output to a file. (4) InnoDB lock waits in MySQL state "statistics" are **not** listed in `INNODB_TRX` here — detect waits through `performance_schema.data_lock_waits` joined to `threads` on `BLOCKING_THREAD_ID` = the holder's `CONNECTION_ID()`. (5) Windows PowerShell 5.1 reads a script without BOM as ANSI — scripts with "—" / "→" must be saved with a UTF-8 BOM (or the text gets mojibake). (6) Maven incremental compile can miss changed main signatures: use `clean test-compile` after constructor changes. (7) Company locks go only through `CompanyIdResolver.lockForUpdate` (primary key); fixtures that delete and re-create the same company code need their own resolver instance (a shared cached id fails closed). (8) `SQLException` is `Iterable` — `assertThat(sql)` is ambiguous in AssertJ; cast to `Object`.
- **P06-T4 correction-round lessons (2026-10-10):** (1) the CP component scan also registers `@TestConfiguration` classes — `LocalKeycloakRealmRegistryIT.LocalRedis` puts `redisConnectionFactory` + `stringRedisTemplate` into every CP test context (bean overriding is on), so a test's own `stringRedisTemplate` is silently replaced: register an unannotated class through `@Import` with a differently named `@Primary StringRedisTemplate` (see `EntitlementTransactionWiringMySqlRedisIT`); (2) re-stubbing with `when(mock.m(any()))` invokes the previous answer with `null` — use `doAnswer(..).when(mock)`; (3) a new `@Scheduled` method fails `CompanyTenancyArchitectureTest` until the COMMON `ScheduledJobPolicy` allow-list has an owner-approved entry (and `ScheduledJobRuleTest` counts the PLATFORM entries); after changing the COMMON test-jar run `mvn -o install -pl COMMON-LIBRARY` before CP-only runs; (4) a negative check of a correction: save a copy of the corrected files, temporarily re-introduce the defect into the repository files themselves, `touch` them, run, restore them byte-identical from the copy (verify with `cmp`) and `touch` again — and describe it exactly so; (5) report every intermediate failed run with its cause and the later re-run, never only the final green numbers; (6) under REPEATABLE READ a transaction's snapshot starts at its first plain read: take the company row lock before any plain read when the work depends on the company's aggregate state (P06-T4 N1).
- **P06-T4 lessons (2026-10-09):** (1) a FAILED audit written in its own transaction must not carry `company_id` while the caller holds the company row lock (FK check waits for the lock → 50 s lock wait, audit lost) — put the company id in the detail; (2) the CP DataJpaTest / IT contexts component-scan the whole application, so `EntitlementRecomputeListener` runs in every CP IT that publishes the subscription / company events — seed entitlements through subscription items (`EntitlementFixtures.subscribe` / `planItem` / `verifiedDatabases`), never by inserting `tbl_company_entitlement` rows directly; (3) `Copy-Item` / `cp -p` keep the old mtime, so Maven does not recompile a restored file — touch it before a negative check; (4) database / FILE modules are published only with a VERIFIED DATABASE resource (O-T4-1).
- CP `@ComponentScan("nyaalkaran.erp.system")` also scans test classes: never put `@Configuration` / `@Component` / `@RestController` into CP tests (use plain classes, `@TestConfiguration`, or the `ControlPlaneSecurityHarness` pattern).
- Spring Security in the CP: use explicit `AntPathRequestMatcher` matchers (`securityMatcher(String...)` needs the MVC introspector bean that `@DataJpaTest` slices lack).
- Since P03-T7 every Spring context of a servlet module needs `erp.m2m.*`: each module has a test-only `src/test/resources/application.properties` (closed loopback port 9, value `test-only`); a new module or test context needs the same. Internal API calls in tests must carry `Authorization: Bearer TestRealms.serviceToken("svc-…")` and a `@TestConfiguration` `@Primary RealmRegistry` from `TestRealms.registry(...)`.
- Filter order in the services: internal-token filters (ACCOUNT-BOOK / CRM / NOTIFICATION) +3, `InternalServiceAuthenticationFilter` +4, `TenantContextFilter` +5, `TenantVerificationFilter` +6, entitlement +7. Refusals carry `X-Error-Code`.
- Stage E architecture tests fail on retired identifiers / hand-built `cmp_` / `co-` names; exclusions only with justification.
- Mockito inline mock of a `synchronized` method + `verify(timeout)` deadlocks the caller thread: poll `mockingDetails(..).getInvocations()` first.
- Spring JWT clock skew: gateway / CP 60 s, services 30 s (expiry tests must wait past it).
- ACCOUNT-BOOK (and FILE `application.yml`) sources are CRLF (keep CRLF; `sed` / `Write` produce LF — convert with perl). In bash, a heredoc containing an apostrophe can break: write the text to a file first.
- In `perl -pi -e '… or die'` scope the `die` to the matching line, otherwise it aborts on line 1.
- Git-Bash `sed -i` on this machine strips CR from CRLF files: edit an LF copy, then write back with `sed 's/$/\r/' copy > file` and compare CR counts with the backup.
- Full IT reactor ≈ 1 h: request keep-awake first (the machine slept once during the 33 s token-expiry wait and the 2 h background limit killed the run). Never leave a shell's working directory inside a module's `target/` (Windows file lock fails `clean`).
- P03-T9: a new `@Scheduled` method fails every module's architecture test unless it has `@RunPerTenant` or an owner-approved `ScheduledJobPolicy` entry.
- P03-T11: identity-sync realms come only from the realm registry (`IdentityRealmTargets`); a company without a registered ACTIVE realm gets no identity / invitation (fail closed). Keycloak 25 admin checks are **token-based**: after a realm is created, the creator needs a fresh token; a client with `fullScopeAllowed: false` needs scope mappings for its realm-management roles. Keycloak 24+ drops unmanaged user attributes unless the realm's user profile allows them.
- `docker compose config` prints every `env_file` value: validate compose only on a scratch copy with a dummy `.env` (P03-T8 method).
- **PHASE_05 / PHASE_06 lessons (2026-10-08):** the P05-T3 finders `PlanRepository.findByPlanTypeOrderByIdAsc` (PESSIMISTIC_WRITE) and `ModuleDependencyRepository.findByStatusForCycleCheck` (PESSIMISTIC_READ) lock — never call them from read-only code ("Cannot execute statement in a READ ONLY transaction"); `Plan.module` / `Plan.bundle` / `BundleModule.module` relations have no getters (use the ids); the CP uses the default MySQL dialect, so `@Enumerated(STRING)` creates native ENUM columns that `ddl-auto: update` never widens on an existing DB (fresh DB = supported schema, E6-6); the legacy `Subscription.planVersionId` still has 6 main callers and 9 test classes.
- **Documentation edits:** the `saas-plan-doc` files are LF (verify by bytes, never insert CRLF); write the edit as a perl script with the Write tool into your scratchpad, use literal `\Q…\E` matches that must hit exactly once, dry-run into the scratchpad first, back up every file before applying, append WORK_LOG entries as LF.
